The Black Bishop is on d5.

Wait, let's check d5b7 again.

From d5 to b7: The bishop is at (4,5) [d5]. b7 is (2,7).
Difference: 2 units left, 2 units up. This is a diagonal move.
So, Black move d5b7 puts the White bishop at a7 under attack.
If White Bishop at a7 captures d5b7? No, Bishop at a7 is on a light square (a7 is white/light? a=1, 7=7. 1+7=8. Even is light. No, a1 is dark. a2 light, a3 dark... a7 is light).
Let's check the square colors.
a1=Dark, b1=Light, c1=Dark, d1=Light, e1=Dark, f1=Light, g1=Dark, h1=Light.
Move up to rank 7:
a1 D -> a2 L -> a3 D -> a4 L -> a5 D -> a6 L -> a7 D.
So a7 is Dark.
d5: d1 L -> d2 D -> d3 L -> d4 D -> d5 L.
So d5 is Light.
Wait, a light-squared bishop cannot attack a dark-squared bishop.
Let's re-verify square colors.
Standard board: a1 is black (dark).
(rank + file) even = dark, odd = light?
a=1, b=2, c=3, d=4, e=5, f=6, g=7, h=8.
a1: 1+1=2 (Dark)
a7: 1+7=8 (Dark)
d5: 4+5=9 (Light)
Yes, they are on opposite colors. d5 is Light sq, a7 is Dark sq.
So Black cannot attack the White bishop with the Black bishop.

c6 is adjacent to c5. Bishops don't attack adjacent squares horizontally/vertically.
d5c6:
The squares controlled by a bishop on c6 are:
(a8, b7, d7, e8) and (a4, b5, d5, e4, f3, g2, h1).

If the bishop moves to c6, it doesn't check the king or block any immediate moves of the king (since it's on a different color).
